## KP Riverside Neonatal ICU Manual › Goal
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/index.html#goal

The goal of this handbook is to centralize clinical protocols and guidelines approved by the neonatologists and NICU staff in one place for easy reference.

## KP Riverside Neonatal ICU Manual › Arrangement
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/index.html#arrangement

This handbook is arranged by into three parts:

- NICU Operations\
- General Care
- System-Based Protocols and Guidelines\
- Special Considerations\
- Health Maintenance
- Appendices

## 3 Physician Operations › 3.4 Daily Rounds Process
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/operations.html#daily-rounds-process

Checklist

- NICU accordion summary
- Review labs
- Review meds
- Review growth and flowsheet
- Revise the problem list
- Orders
- E&M coding
- [Progress note](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/documentation.html#sec-daily-progress-note)
- Call parents to provide update, or update at bedside

## 3 Physician Operations › 3.5 Quality Management › Important notes:
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/operations.html#important-notes

1.  P scoring is based on POTENTIAL FOR HARM.
2.  P2 is the serious and will require corrective actions.
3.  If you receive P2, you can appeal and ask for independent review.
4.  Standard for care = is the COMMUNITY STANDARD, not department standard.
5.  It’s ok to discuss the case with the physician under review to get their thought process.
6.  Any learning experience will be shared to the Neo group without naming the physician under review.
7.  Any request for case review can be submitted to me or directly to QM regardless of department, e.g radiology, nursing, peds etc.

## 3 Physician Operations › 3.6 Interdepartmental Agreements › Escalation process
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/operations.html#escalation-process

Each agreement is reviewed on a yearly basis by its agreement owners. Non-compliance with an agreement is escalated on a case-by-case basis and in real time, per the *Patient Care Issue Resolution Process*:

| Step | Action |
| --- | --- |
| Service issue (peer-to-peer) | Have a conversation with physician or peer (involve a manager if you need to) |
| Resolution Level 1 | Involve Manager or Director |
| Resolution Level 2 | Involve Physician Leader, Physician-in-Charge, or Chief |
| Resolution Level 3 | Involve AAMD, Area Administrator, or COO |
| Resolution Level 4 | Involve AMD, CAO, or Executive Director |

## 3 Physician Operations › 3.6 Interdepartmental Agreements › Radiology
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/operations.html#sec-agreement-radiology

Service Level Agreement (SLA), Riverside Medical Center. Agreement owners: Dr. Heather Le (Chief of Diagnostic Imaging Services), Sukhvinder Puri (Director of Diagnostic Imaging Services), Dr. James Washington (Chief of NICU Services, Riverside area), Maria Rodriguez (Director of NICU Services, Riverside area). Signed July–September 2026.

| Study | Agreement |
| --- | --- |
| IV contrast studies (CT/MRI/MRA/MRV as examples) | Radiology techs to be trained to inject contrast for NICU studies only for babies with peripheral IV access (trained to scope of practice) |
| Emergent UGI to rule out malrotation | Okay with upper GI/SBFT during work hours, M–F 8 am to 5 pm. Other situations transfer out |
| VCUG | Should be available at RMC/MVMC, M–F 9 am to 4 pm. Teams and email Dr. Le for real-time escalations |
| RUS with doppler study | Transfer out if the study is needed for a NICU baby |
| MRA/MRV/MRI of the body | Transfer to pediatric institute of excellence if these studies are needed |
| MRI without contrast of head and brainstem | Can be performed at both RMC and MV with confirmed compatible EKG and respiratory machines |

**Real-time escalation:** contact the radiologist on call; Teams and email the Radiology Chief; Radiology manager on call.

## 3 Physician Operations › 3.6 Interdepartmental Agreements › Pediatrics–Neonatology: FCC Coverage
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/operations.html#sec-agreement-fcc

Pediatrics–Neonatology FCC Coverage Interdepartmental Agreement, Riverside Service Area.

We share responsibility for providing an outstanding care experience for our new parents, outstanding clinical care for our newborns, and an outstanding working experience for our team. As a team, we will proactively identify potential needs/problems and suggest solutions. We will communicate to the team if we observe something team members may do to make the day more efficient, take better care of our members or each other. Continuous role clarification is encouraged to enhance partnership of team.

## 3 Physician Operations › 3.6 Interdepartmental Agreements › Pediatrics–Neonatology: FCC Coverage › Moreno Valley (MVMC) FCC
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/operations.html#moreno-valley-mvmc-fcc

**Pediatrician FCC coverage: 8 AM – 12 PM**

- Complete the discharge summary/order for newborns discharged before 12 PM. Write a conditional order/discharge summary for newborns that are being discharged after 12 PM. The pediatrician will communicate with the on-call neonatologist items that will need to be followed prior to discharge.
- Complete the H&Ps for newborns that were delivered after 8 PM the night prior and for newborns that have been delivered by 10:00 AM.
- Perform all circumcisions.
- Not responsible for attending deliveries.
- Will sign out to the neonatologist at 12 PM if there are any items pending of patients that are being discharged after 12 PM and for the other newborns in which laboratory/radiological studies are pending. The pending items will be noted in the “Write Handoff” section in Health Connect.
- Prioritize discharges, circumcisions, then progress notes and H&Ps.
- Work that is not completed will be signed out to the neonatologists (if this occurs, usually will be the H&Ps).
- Pediatricians will place future outpatient orders for hip ultrasounds, renal ultrasounds, thyroid labs, etc.
- If possible, pediatrician can attend board rounds in Moreno Valley at 8:30 to discuss any issues/questions, concerns and patient management.
- Pediatrician can contact the neonatologist via the most convenient method for the pediatrician (Vocera, paging or calling their office/call room).

**Neonatologist FCC coverage: 12 PM – 8 AM**

- Follow up on the newborns being discharged after 12 PM with pending items.
- Will need to complete the discharge order/summary for newborns that were not anticipated to be discharged. This should happen rarely.
- Complete the H&Ps for newborns that are delivered from 10:00 AM to 8 PM.
- Will be responsible for managing any abnormal results from the hypoglycemia protocol and for serial TCBs for ABO incompatibility.
- If nursery nurse has concerns about an infant on FCC, they are to call the neonatologist, who will determine if the NICU team/RN can first evaluate the infant.
- Will need to follow up on serum bilirubin for patients seen in Moreno Valley Great Starts clinic when results are not back by 5 PM. Based on the result, may need to order Bili Blanket, repeat serum bilirubin, and schedule a follow-up with PCP or another pediatrician.
- Neonatologist is expected to write the late preterm admission orders if asked by nursing for late preterm infants who are in observation in the NICU in Riverside and Moreno Valley. If not under observation, the pediatrician is expected to write the late preterm admission orders.
- Neonatologist is expected to write a note for any infant in observation in the NICU, for infants requiring resuscitation, and for deliveries they attended in person (physical exam by neonatologist is not necessarily required on the infants that transition back to FCC).
- If anticipatory discharge is cancelled, neonatologist is expected to cancel the discharge order and leave a note or write handoff with the reason for cancellation.
- Neonatologists can attend board rounds in Moreno Valley at 8:30 to discuss any issues/questions, concerns and patient management.
- Neonatologists are not expected to perform circumcision consents but can do so if time allows, as it is helpful to the pediatrician’s workflow.
- Neonatologist is expected to provide discussion and anticipatory guidance with the parents on hospital-specific diagnoses such as congenital hydronephrosis, late preterm infants required to stay 48 hours, labs due to maternal Graves disease, staying for 24 hours for Accuchecks (SGA, LGA and GDM infants) and TCB checks for ABO/Rh incompatibility, discussing nirsevimab with parents who refused at birth, etc.
- Neonatologists are responsible for all the critical lab results from the regional lab between 7:30 PM – 6:30 AM (this is currently being managed by the Moreno Valley ED team).
- Neonatologists are to manage bilirubin labs from MV Great Starts and Palm Desert patients that result after 5 PM (these are not to be managed by the Riverside DOD or ED team).
- Neonatologists are responsible for pharmacy calls from 7:30 PM – 6:30 AM (currently being managed by the MoVal ED).
- May be consulted by the Emergency Department to assist in the management of children less than 30 days of age.
- Will sign out to the pediatrician at 8 AM if there are any urgent items that need follow-up. The pending items will be noted in the “Write Handoff” section in Health Connect. Discuss with the incoming pediatrician the plan of treatment regarding the critical labs that were received.
- Pediatrician can contact the neonatologist via the most convenient method for the pediatrician (Vocera, paging or calling their office/call room).

## 3 Physician Operations › 3.6 Interdepartmental Agreements › Pediatrics–Neonatology: FCC Coverage › Riverside (RMC) FCC
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/operations.html#riverside-rmc-fcc

**Pediatrician FCC coverage: 7 AM – 7 PM**

- Complete the discharge summary/order for newborns discharged before 7:30 PM. Write a conditional order/discharge summary for newborns that are being discharged after 7:30 PM. The pediatrician will communicate with the neonatologist items that will need to be followed prior to discharge.
- Complete the H&Ps for all newborns.
- Perform all circumcisions.
- Available for emergency room consults (note is expected; please contact hospitalist directly if admission is recommended).
- Available for Urgent Care consults.
- Not responsible for attending deliveries.
- Responsible for critical lab results between 6:30 AM – 7:30 PM (except for MoVal Great Starts and Palm Desert bili labs).
- Responsible for general pharmacy calls between 6:30 AM – 7:30 PM.
- Responsible for Home Health calls (not NICU related).
- Will sign out to the neonatologist at 7:30 PM if there are any items pending for patients that are being discharged after 12 PM and for the other newborns in which laboratory/radiological studies are pending. The pending items will be noted in the “Write Handoff” section in Health Connect.

**Neonatologist FCC coverage: 7 PM – 7 AM**

- Follow up on the newborns being discharged after 7:30 PM with pending items.
- Will need to complete the discharge order/summary for newborns that were not anticipated to be discharged.
- Will be responsible for managing any abnormal results from the hypoglycemia protocol and for serial TCBs for ABO incompatibility.
- Neonatologist is expected to write the late preterm admission orders if asked by nursing for late preterm infants who are in observation in the NICU in Riverside and Moreno Valley. If not under observation, the pediatrician is expected to write the late preterm admission orders.
- Neonatologist is expected to write late preterm admission orders for infants born between 7:30 PM – 6:30 AM.
- Neonatologist is expected to write a note for any infant in observation in the NICU, for infants requiring resuscitation, and for deliveries they attended in person.
- May be consulted by the Emergency Department to assist in the management of children less than 30 days of age.
- Will sign out to the pediatrician at 8 AM if there are any urgent items that need follow-up. The pending items will be noted in the “Write Handoff” section in Health Connect.

## 3 Physician Operations › 3.6 Interdepartmental Agreements › Pediatrics–Neonatology: FCC Coverage › Shared clinical agreements
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/operations.html#shared-clinical-agreements

**Hyperbilirubinemia**

- Agreement among the pediatricians and neonatologists for newborns with hyperbilirubinemia risk factors (such as ABO incompatibility) to initiate phototherapy 2 below phototherapy threshold based on [bilitool.org](https://bilitool.org) or [peditools.org](https://peditools.org).
- In certain cases, infants who can no longer remain on FCC due to maternal discharge, but still need phototherapy, may need admission to the NICU.
- If the infant is on full phototherapy, the pediatric group can transition to a bili-blanket and order a follow-up bilirubin 6–8 hours later.
- If rate of rise (ROR) is > 0.2/hr, then admit to the NICU as they have failed the bili-blanket.
- If ROR is < 0.2/hr, then OK to discharge home with bili-blanket and follow up in 1 day with repeat total serum bilirubin prior to their appointment.
- If mother has been discharged and there is space on postpartum, OK for infant to stay on FCC service while in FCC and under phototherapy.
- If there is bed shortage, infant would then need to be transferred to NICU for management (under NICU service).
- Please reference the 2022 AAP guidelines on management of hyperbilirubinemia.

**Maternal Graves/hyperthyroidism**

- For infants born to mothers with history of Graves disease or hyperthyroidism, please initiate thyroid labs ASAP per algorithm and consult with Endocrinology as necessary.
- Please reference the MVH Neo/FCC Team channel for the algorithm.

**Positive maternal UDS**

- For mothers with positive THC on UDS (no other drug positive), do NOT need to order UDS on baby.

**Trisomy 21 infants**

- Any unstable infant will be admitted to the NICU directly.
- Team huddle for these infants, to include the pediatrician, neonatologist, charge nurses and bedside nurses.
- If they fail 3 feedings, transfer to NICU (with flexibility if close).
- Monitor these infants at least 48–72 hours on FCC prior to discharge.
- If mother is being discharged and infant not ready, they will be transferred to the NICU.
- Multiple major congenital anomalies will be admitted to the NICU. If NICU and Peds do not agree on this admission, Peds can ask for a formal consult.
- Infant does not need to be seen by OT prior to discharge if they are feeding well (as OT only comes one day/week).

Pediatrician/neonatologist:

- Evaluate Trisomy 21 or suspected Trisomy 21 babies soon after birth and talk to parents about the diagnosis or suspected diagnosis.
- Please be responsive to nurse or family concerns.
- Trisomy 21 workflow: TSH, echocardiogram, chromosomes, genetics consult outpatient, etc. Neonatologist/peds is expected to order the echo at the time of exam and H&P to expedite the study taking place (not urgent, only for it to be done next day).
- In the first 12–24 hours, the nurse caring for these infants should have a 2 couplet ratio (max 3). If this ratio is not possible due to staffing issues, infant should go to NICU.
- Social work consult, reason: IRC referral.

**Problem list: common diagnoses**

- Newborn, single liveborn, in hospital by vaginal delivery
- Newborn, single liveborn, in hospital by cesarean section
- Newborn, single liveborn, born outside of hospital prior to admission
- Circumcision Consultation (or Elective Circumcision)
- Immunization Counseling
- Prophylactic Immunotherapy for Respiratory Syncytial Virus
- SGA
- LGA
- Infant of Diabetic Mother
- ABO incompatibility reaction
- Rh incompatibility reaction
- Prematurity of Newborn, completed 36, 35, 34 weeks… or Preterm infant, unspecified completed weeks gestation
- Screening for congenital hip dislocation, breech presentation
- Screening for congenital dislocation of hip (for multiple births)
- Screening for thyroid disorder (for twins, Trisomy 21, etc.)
- Congenital Hydronephrosis

Under the diagnosis of *Newborn, single liveborn…*:

- Please include GA, mother’s blood type and GBS status, and infant’s blood type if done.
- Can also mention any other relevant information, such as history of maternal depression/anxiety, mother with pre-eclampsia, oligo, etc.
- There is a smart phrase some physicians use to autopopulate this: `.birth` or `.birth1` (can copy from the smartphrase list of Dr. Garabedian, Dr. Song or Dr. Chen).

**Outpatient bilirubin management**

- You get TSB calls from labs done at the Moreno Valley hospital, which includes Iris Great Starts and the Palm Desert labs.
- Please call parent with the result and plan, which can be no further follow-up, repeat the bili the next day, etc.
- Please order any follow-up TSB “stat”.
  - Stat laboratories are Iris, Murrieta and Riverside (not Heacock).
  - Please let parent know which specific lab to go to. If they go to the Heacock lab it will not be run stat and will result after 5–9 PM.
- Ordering the biliblanket: order is **“phototherapy, light, including supplies”**.
  - Apria will deliver to their house within 4 hours of order.
  - If after 5 PM, can call the ER Case Manager to let them know of the order, and they can call Apria to confirm they received the order.
  - Can also inform parent that if they don’t receive the biliblanket within 4–6 hours, to call the L&D secretary to speak with the neonatologist on call.
- Med Advice: can call them or send them a message to book a follow-up in-person appointment or TAV.
  - Routing pool is **“P Pedririv Med Advice” (10139)**
  - Med Advice phone number: 951-353-4354
  - Weekend follow-up for bili: can book into Riverside or Murrieta clinics for in-person follow-up. Does not need to wait until Monday.

**Anticipatory discharges**

- If mother is staying, discharge order needs to be cancelled for infant, with a note acknowledging the cancelled discharge and reason.
- If a parent decides they want to be discharged after the pediatrician has left, neonatologist can discharge the infant if all criteria are met.
  - Great Starts clinic is available on weekdays.
  - On weekends or holidays, we book into the Pediatric “After Hours” clinic, which is in Riverside and Murrieta.
- Conditional discharge note (also in [Section B.2.2.7](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/dotPhrases.html#sec-dotphrase-fcc-conditional-discharge)):

> Infant may be discharged home today after \*\*\* once all of the following criteria have been met:\
> A. Vital Signs are within Normal Limits (Temp 36.5–37.9 °C, HR 100–160, RR less than 60)\
> B. Infant has voided and passed stool\
> C. Infant has passed CCHD screening with O2 sat greater than 94%\
> D. Newborn screening is complete\
> E. Hearing Screening is complete; if referred, CMV buccal swab has been obtained and outpatient audiology follow-up has been scheduled\
> F. Hepatitis B vaccine has been given, and if indicated, HBIG has been given. If parents refused hep B vaccine, declination waiver has been signed.\
> G. Transcutaneous bilirubin levels have not exceeded the threshold to require a serum bili level\
> H. If infant is being evaluated for hypoglycemia, all blood sugars have been completed and infant has not required any dextrose gel OR infant has had 3 serial glucose POCT levels greater than or equal to 45 mg/dL.\
> I. Infant has had a recent repeat weight, with ≤ 6% weight loss from birth weight on day 1 or ≤ 8% weight loss from birth weight on day 2 or 3. If breastfeeding, a LATCH score of 8 or more is documented and mother has demonstrated ability to latch baby independently OR mother has worked with lactation and a discharge feeding plan has been documented.\
> J. Follow-up appointment(s) scheduled per unit policy: 1) in Great Starts in 2 days, 2) at 3 weeks with PCP, and 3) at 2 months with PCP.\
> If above criteria have not been met then please cancel discharge and notify MD on call.

**Miscellaneous**

- Please document red reflex in your H&P if it can be done. If not done, please write it in sign-out so the pediatrician is aware.
- Please contact any of us with questions; we are all happy to help.
- Can also call the DOD in Riverside with questions.

# 4 Research/QI activities
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/publications.html

## 4 Research/QI activities › 4.1 QI Projects › Breast Before Bottle (2024; PI: Dr. Kasee Houston) › Summary
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/publications.html#summary

**The goal is for our infant’s *first* PO feeding to be a direct breast/chestfeeding rather than a bottle feed.** Lactating parents that are interested will be able to provide the first oral feed as a direct breast/chestfeed, and the parent will have 24 hours to complete this once the infant is determined to be ready to feed by mouth (after which we will not delay oral feeding and will introduce bottles). A breast/chest before bottle card will be placed on the infant’s isolette to identify these infants, and nurses will arrange for this to be completed.

Babies that are < 33weeks, on >2LHFNC or that have other contraindications to breastmilk will not be included. .

After the breast/chestfeed, supplementation PG or PO will be determined based on the quality and time of the chestfeed, ranging from no supplementation to full volume supplementation (see chart on flyer). You may use your discretion to adjust feeds as needed.

## 5 NICU Admission › 5.2 Central line placement › UVC Placement
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/admission.html#uvc-placement

Criteria for UVC:

``` {.mermaid .mermaid-js}
flowchart TD

A["Birth GA"] --> B["<28 weeks"]
A --> C["&ge;28 weeks"]
B --> D["UVC placement"]
C --> E["Birth weight"]
E --> F["<1,250g"]
F --> D
E --> G["&ge;1,250g"]
G --> H["<div align='left'>Clinically indicated:<ul><li>Severe respiratory disease</li><li>Clinically unstable</li><li>IUGR</li>etc...</ul></div>"]
H --> D
```

Initial fluid

- Port 1:
  - D10AA or D5AA
    - 0.5U/mL heparin at 1mL/hr
    - 1U/mL heparin at 0.5mL/hr
- Port 2:
  - NaAce 77mEq/L or 154mEq/L
    - 1U/mL heparin at 0.3mL/hr
    - 1U/mL heparin at 0.5mL/hr
    - 0.5U/mL heparin at 1mL/hr

Minimum KVO rates and heparin concentration by line: see [Section 12.2.4](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/nutrition-fluid.html#sec-line-kvo).

Any time an infant is moved from the ED to the NICU, you must place an ‘**Admit to Inpatient**’ order (Order #244425).

## 5 NICU Admission › 5.4 Care of the Safely Surrendered Infant
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/admission.html#sec-safe-surrender

The provider will consider the following guidelines (if not already completed) when admitting the Safely Surrendered Infant:

1.  Obtain newborn screen. Complete paperwork with the name and demographic information of the CPS worker. On the Comments section of the paperwork, indicate Safe Surrender. If the screen has already been completed, the Genetics Division needs to be notified to change the contact person’s name to the CPS worker.
2.  Obtain urine drug screen – initiate neonatal abstinence scoring as indicated.
3.  Obtain total bilirubin at least once, and repeat if infants is jaundiced.
4.  Obtain additional labs as indicated: ABO/Rh, direct Coombs test, random glucose blood culture, CBC/D, HBsAg, RPR, HIV rapid test.
5.  Medications:
    a.  Phytonadione 1mg IM if the administration status is unknown.
    b.  Erythromycin 0.5% ophthalmic ointment, 1cm to both eyes if the administration status is unknown.
    c.  Hepatitis B vaccine 0.5mL (10mcg) intramuscularly to right thigh if the administration status is unknown.
    d.  HBIG 0.5mL intramuscularly to left thigh if indicated. CPS worker’s information is to be included in the state Perinatal Hepatitis B Prevention Program form.
6.  Perform a hearing screen if the infant has been admitted to the NICU.
7.  Any significant medical history obtained from the Medical History Questionnaire will be entered manually into the Safely Surrendered Infant’s Problem List/medical record. If known, significant maternal laboratory results may be manually entered on the Safely Surrendered Infant’s medical record with the consent of the mother.
8.  If the Safely Surrendered Infant was already receiving care in a hospital unit such as NICU, Postpartum, or FCC, essential components of the infant’s history may be recorded on the Safely Surrendered Infant’s chart without reference to the mother.
9.  Any necessary consent must be obtained from CPS unless they are for urgent/emergent procedures.

When the Safely Surrendered Infant is discharged, discharge the infant into the care of CPS (and adoptive mother if applicable) following established policies. Provide the following paperwork to CPS and contact Social Services:

1.  After Visit Summary (AVS).
2.  Include on the AVS instructions that a copy of the Safely Surrendered Infant’s medical record may be obtained through Medical Records.
3.  Copy of the completed Medical History Questionnaire, if available.
4.  Photocopy of the Safely Surrender Bracelet.

## 6 Transfer an Infant Out › 6.1 CCS Information for Transferring › CCS Criteria for Critically Ill Neonates and Infants › Critically Ill Neonates
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/transfer.html#critically-ill-neonates

Neonates who do not have an identified CCS-eligible condition but require one or more of the following services in a CCS-approved NICU:

- Invasive or non-invasive positive pressure ventilatory assistance
- Supplemental oxygen concentration by hood of ≥40%
- UAC or Peripheral Art Line
- UVC, Central Venous Catheter, PICC
- Peripheral Line for pharmacological support of the cardiovascular support
- TPN – Central or Peripheral
- Chest Tube

## 6 Transfer an Infant Out › 6.1 CCS Information for Transferring › CCS Criteria for Critically Ill Neonates and Infants › Neonates and Infants
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/transfer.html#neonates-and-infants

Neonates and infants who do not have an identified CCS-eligible condition but require 2 or more of the following services in a CCS-approved NICU:

- Supplemental inspired oxygen
- Peripheral IV Line for meds, blood products, and/or meds for cardiac support
- Caffeine: Pharmacological support for apnea and/or bradycardia episodes
- Tube feedings

# 7 Discharging an Infant
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.1 General Discharge Criteria (preliminary) › Respiratory
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#respiratory

- In room air for at least 24 hrs, or
- If discharged home on oxygen:
  - All DME and supplies have been delivered, and parental education has been completed by the vendor
  - CXR and blood gas within 1 week of discharge date available in baby’s chart

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.1 General Discharge Criteria (preliminary) › Dietary
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#dietary

- Off IV fluid with post-IVF glucose levels within age-appropriate normal range
- On-demand feeds of at least 150ml/kg/day for 24 hrs if CGA ≥ 36 weeks, or 48 hrs if CGA < 36 weeks
- On discharge feeding regimen for at least 24 hrs

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.1 General Discharge Criteria (preliminary) › Thermoregulation
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#thermoregulation

- In open crib swaddled with one layer of clothes for at least 24 hrs with normal temperature

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.2 Discharge Diet › Weight-based diet for discharge › Considerations for Discharge diet:
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#considerations-for-discharge-diet

1.  Breast Milk with Human Milk Fortifier (HMF):

    - Fortified breast milk with HMF should be prioritized as the primary source of nutrition whenever possible to support optimal growth and development.

2.  Fortified Feeds:

    - If exclusive breastfeeding is not feasible, the discharge diet may include:

      - Similac Neosure or Enfamil Enfacare (available in retail stores)

      - Specialty Formulas: SSC24 or SSC30 (not available in retail stores)

3.  Case-by-Case Evaluation:

    - The discharge diet plan will be individualized based on the infant’s growth trajectory, feeding tolerance, medical history, and nutritional requirements.

    - Adjustments will be finalized 24 hours prior to discharge to ensure sufficient time to test the discharge regimen in the NICU for tolerance.

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.2 Discharge Diet › Weight-based diet for discharge › 22KCal for BW 1,800g - 2,500g
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#kcal-for-bw-1800g---2500g

##### *Parents may consider one of the following feeding options:*

##### *Option 1*: Exclusively Breast Milk Feeding:

Provide 4 breast milk feeds fortified with Human Milk Fortifier (HMF) to 22kcal/oz per day. The remaining feeds can be either direct nursing or expressed breast milk (EBM).

- 4 cases of Human Milk Fortifier (HMF) will be provided at time of discharge. Add 1 packet of HMF to every 50 ml of breast milk to make 22 kcal.
- Once the HMF is depleted, switch to Neosure powder or Enfacare powder to fortify breast milk to 22 kcal, at the discretion of the primary care provider.

##### *Option 2*: Mix of Formula and Breast Milk Feeding:

Give 3 feeds of Neosure 22Kcal daily. The remaining feeds can be either direct nursing, expressed breast milk, or additional Neosure/Enfacare, depending on the mother’s milk supply.

- 2 cases of Neosure formula will be provided at discharge, which may last for 1-2 months, based on the volume used.
- Neosure can be purchased over the counter if needed, at the discretion of the primary care provider.

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.2 Discharge Diet › Weight-based diet for discharge › 24KCal for BW < 1,800g
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#kcal-for-bw-1800g

##### *Parents may choose one of the following feeding options*:

##### *Option 1*: Exclusively Breast Milk Feeding:

Provide **4-6** breast milk feeds fortified with Human Milk Fortifier (HMF) to 24kcal/oz per day. The remaining feeds can be either direct nursing or expressed breast milk (EBM).

- **4-6** cases of Human Milk Fortifier (HMF) will be provided at time of discharge. Add 2 packets of HMF to every 50 ml of breast milk to make 24 kcal.
- After the HMF is depleted, switch to Neosure powder or Enfacare powder to fortify breast milk to 24 kcal, at the discretion of the primary care provider.

##### *Option 2*: Mix of Formula and Breast Milk Feeding:

Give 3 feeds of SSC30 daily. The remaining feeds can be either direct nursing, expressed breast milk, or Neosure/Enfacare depending on the mother’ milk supply (caloric density range 23.7- 25kcal).

- 4 cases of SSC30 and 1 case of Neosure22 will be provided at discharge. These supplies may last for 2-3 months. Parents can purchase additional Neosure/Enfacare at retail store while the baby is receiving SSC30.

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.2 Discharge Diet › Instruction for PCP
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#instruction-for-pcp

If an infant continues to require 24Kcal after depleting SSC30 or HMF (at the discretion of the PCP), fortify with Neosure or Enfacare powder to maintain 24Kcal. If 24Kcal is no longer necessary, begin weaning to 22Kcal for 1-2 months, then reduce to 20Kcal once the baby’s actual weight reaches 3500 gm or at the 25^th^ percentile for weight, length, and head circumference.

The recipes will be structured as below:

**22Kcal feeds using Neosure or Enfacare powder:**

- Add 1 teaspoon unpacked, level of Neosure or Enfacare powder to 5 oz of breastmilk to make 22KCal.\
- Add 1 unpacked, level scoops of Neosure or Enfacre powder to 2 oz of water to make 22Kcal.

**24Kcal feeds using Neosure or Enfacare powder:**

- Add 2 teaspoons unpacked, level of Neosure or Enfacare powder to 5 oz of breastmilk to make 24Kcal.\
- Add 5 unpacked, level scoops of Neosure or Enfacre powder to 9 oz of water to make 24Kcal.\
- Add 1/2 teaspoon of Neosure or Enfacare powder to 3 oz of ready-to-feed Neosure/Enfacare to make 24Kcal.

Note

Enfamil Enfacare formula 22KCal is comparable to Neosure 22KCal formula. It would be helpful to include Enfamil Enfacare as an option for parents. in the event, parents cannot procure Neosure supply at retail stores, they can obtain Enfamil Enfacare as a solution.

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.2 Discharge Diet › Term infants
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#term-infants

Do not need to test infants on regular infant formula unless clinically indicated (e.g., specialized formula) prior to discharge from the NICU.

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.3 Donor Breast Milk – Home Ordering Information
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#sec-donor-milk-home-ordering

[Download Donor Breast Milk - Home Ordering Information](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/files/donor-milk-home-ordering.pdf)

- **Ordering Without Prescription**: Families can contact milk banks directly to order up to 40 ounces of donor milk without a prescription.
- **Additional Milk**: If more donor milk is needed, families should follow up with their pediatrician.
- **Eligibility**: All patients are eligible to receive a maximum of 40 ounces without a prescription by contacting the milk bank and completing electronic consent forms.
- **Mothers’ Milk Bank Contact**:
  - Email: <recipient.coordinator@mothersmilk.org>
  - Phone: (408) 638-2822
  - Website: <https://mothersmilk.org/request-milk/for-a-newborn/>
- **Ni-Q Contact**:
  - Phone: 844-305-7674
  - Electronic Form: <https://www.ni-q.com/direct-to-family/> (response within 24 hours)
- **Prescription for More Than 40 Ounces**: Patients needing more than 40 ounces must have a doctor complete the San Jose Mothers’ Milk Bank Prescription form, specific to the milk bank and attached to this document.
- **Insurance Coverage**:
  - San Jose Milk Bank is contracted with Tri-Care and Medi-Cal insurance only.
  - Patients with Tri-Care or Medi-Cal may receive Pasteurized Donor Human Milk (PDHM) via insurance, but prior authorization is required.
  - Patients with other insurance providers will pay out of pocket and can discuss possible reimbursement with their provider.
- **Delivery**:
  - Patients can schedule overnight delivery to their homes.
  - Mothers’ Milk Bank ships nationwide, with a cutoff for overnight deliveries at 11 AM.

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.5 Circumcision › Timing
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#timing

- May be performed by the on-call Family Care Center pediatrician if the infant is < 30 days old.
- Circumcision usually takes place the day before or on the day of NICU discharge.
- If the infant is ≥ 30 days old, the procedure will be performed by a urologist as outpatient.
  - Route note to the following nursing pool: P OBGRIRIV CIRC (P 19729).\
  - There must be a statement stating that the patient is medically cleared for circumcision.\
  - The patient must have received IM Vitamin K prior to the procedure. **Oral Vitamin K will not be accepted**.\
- **Dr. Josephine Hidalgo-Tamola** may be willing to operate up to 3 months of age.

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.6 RSV Prevention after Discharge › nirsevemab (Beyfortus®) information › Counseling highlight
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#counseling-highlight

- We have had a monoclonal antibody to protect from RSV for more than 20 years, but it was expensive and only approved for high risk infants.
- The new drug will be more widely available, but it is in short supply and high demand.
- It is an injection that protects from an infection, it is NOT technically a vaccine, because it does not provoke a response from the immune system:
  - Down side: the protection is not durable
  - Up side: side effects are very few - no fevers have been reported from nirsevimab, not have any severe reactions been seen
- If parents don’t take advantage of the opportunity to get nirsevimab in the hospital, it is very likely that we will NOT have it available later on in clinic.\
- It is NOT a human blood product like IVIG. It is produced in Chinese Hamster ovary cells. This should be acceptable to Jehovah’s Witnesses.

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.6 RSV Prevention after Discharge › nirsevemab (Beyfortus®) information › Consenting & administration
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#consenting-administration

NICU

- Parental informed consent required.
- In general, administer within 24 h of discharge.
- If given on the day of NICU discharge, monitor for 2 hrs prior to disharge.

FCC

- No written or documented consent is required - administration of nirsevimab is covered by the Conditions of Admission that is signed at the time of hospitalization.\
- Verbal consent is required from parents/guardians (similar to vitamin K or Hep B vaccine).
- Administer on the day of FCC discharge. No observation required after administration.

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.6 RSV Prevention after Discharge › Criteria for Synagis › AAP guidelines
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#aap-guidelines

Pediatrics (2014) 134 (2): 415–420.\
<https://doi.org/10.1542/peds.2014-1665>

Summary of Guidance (direct copy/paste from the above source)

- In the first year of life, palivizumab prophylaxis is recommended for infants born before 29 weeks, 0 days’ gestation.

- Palivizumab prophylaxis is not recommended for otherwise healthy infants born at or after 29 weeks, 0 days’ gestation.

- In the first year of life, palivizumab prophylaxis is recommended for preterm infants with CLD of prematurity, defined as birth at <32 weeks, 0 days’ gestation and a requirement for >21% oxygen for at least 28 days after birth.

- Clinicians may administer palivizumab prophylaxis in the first year of life to certain infants with hemodynamically significant heart disease.

- Clinicians may administer up to a maximum of 5 monthly doses of palivizumab (15 mg/kg per dose) during the RSV season to infants who qualify for prophylaxis in the first year of life. Qualifying infants born during the RSV season may require fewer doses. For example, infants born in January would receive their last dose in March.

- Palivizumab prophylaxis is not recommended in the second year of life except for children who required at least 28 days of supplemental oxygen after birth and who continue to require medical intervention (supplemental oxygen, chronic corticosteroid, or diuretic therapy).

- Monthly prophylaxis should be discontinued in any child who experiences a breakthrough RSV hospitalization.

- Children with pulmonary abnormality or neuromuscular disease that impairs the ability to clear secretions from the upper airways may be considered for prophylaxis in the first year of life.

- Children younger than 24 months who will be profoundly immunocompromised during the RSV season may be considered for prophylaxis.

- Insufficient data are available to recommend palivizumab prophylaxis for children with cystic fibrosis or Down syndrome.

- The burden of RSV disease and costs associated with transport from remote locations may result in a broader use of palivizumab for RSV prevention in Alaska Native populations and possibly in selected other American Indian populations.

- Palivizumab prophylaxis is not recommended for prevention of health care-associated RSV disease.

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.8 Newborn Humerus Fracture: Discharge Instructions › Dot phrase: .humerusfx
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#dot-phrase-.humerusfx

Add **`.humerusfx`** (see [Section B.2.2.6](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/dotPhrases.html#sec-dotphrase-humerusfx)) to the discharge patient instructions:

Go to discharge navigator → select **Patient Instructions** in the AVS documentation on the left menu bar → type `.humerusfx`

The handout can also be sent to the family via kp.org.

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.8 Newborn Humerus Fracture: Discharge Instructions › Handout content (.humerusfx)
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#handout-content-.humerusfx

**Optimal position for baby’s arm:**

- At side, with elbow comfortably bent
- Limit to as little to no movement of both shoulder and elbow as possible, until cleared by **Orthopedics**

**Movement will be limited to:**

- Cleaning underarms (see directions below)
- Changing cotton pads every shift and as needed
- Checking for skin breakdown every shift (if home, check for skin breakdown twice daily, AM and PM)

**To move baby’s arm for cleaning:**

- Support entire arm gently
- Maintain elbow bent as shown in picture
- With the arm supported, move the elbow and shoulder **as one unit** to open the side/armpit slightly
- Do skin check and cares as needed
- Place cotton pad/soft cotton gauze between arm and side to prevent skin breakdown. Change pad 2x daily and as needed

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.8 Newborn Humerus Fracture: Discharge Instructions › Positioning
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#positioning

Additional positioning guidance from NICU PT (added to the patient instructions in addition to `.humerusfx`):

- OK to position baby on back and opposite sides
- Avoid side lying over fracture arm
- Opposite arm: allow functional movement — do not overstretch
- Position arm GENTLY across body

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.9 Inland Regional Center Early Start Program › Eligibility criteria for children ages 0-36 months: › Developmental delay
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#developmental-delay

A significant delay is a 33% delay in one or more developmental areas. the developmental areas are: cognitive, communication, physical/motor, social/emotional and adaptive skills.

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.9 Inland Regional Center Early Start Program › Eligibility criteria for children ages 0-36 months: › Established risk
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#established-risk

A condition which has a high probability of resulting in a developmental delay such as Down syndrome or cerebral palsy.

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.9 Inland Regional Center Early Start Program › Eligibility criteria for children ages 0-36 months: › High risk
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#high-risk

A combination of **two or more** medical factors that requires early intervention services based on evaluation and assessment. Some of these factors include (*list not exhaustive*)

- Prematurity < 32 weeks gestation and/or low birth weight of < 1,500 grams.\
- Assisted ventilation for 48 hours or longer during the first 28 days of life.\
- Prenatal substance exposure.\
- Infants born to a parent with a developmental disability.\
- Total pre-phototherapy bilirubin level >20mg/dL

[https://www.inlandrc.org](https://www.inlandrc.org)

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.9 Inland Regional Center Early Start Program › Short list of criteria
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#short-list-of-criteria

Any of the following:

- Hypoglycemia with IV > 48 hrs ?
- Bilirubin > 20 mg/dL
- Intubated for > 24 hrs
- APGAR <5 at 5 mins

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.10 High-Risk Infant Follow-Up › Eligibility criteria (long list)
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#eligibility-criteria-long-list

All infants with birth weight ≤ 1,500g or birth GA < 32 weeks meet criteria.

For infnats with birth weight > 1,500g and birth GA ≥ 32 weeks, one of the following criteria is required:

- pH < 7.0 on an umbilical blood sample or a blood gas obtained within 1 hr of life.
- Apgar score ≤ 3 at 5 mins or < 5 at 10 mins.
- An unstable infant manifested by hypoxia, acidemia, hypoglycemia, and/or hypotension requiring pressor support.
- Persistent apnea which required caffeine or other stimulant medication for the treatment of apnea at discharge.
- Required oxygen for more than 29 days of hospital stay and had radiographic finding consistent with chrnonic lung disease (CLD).
- Infants placed on ECMO.
- Infants who received **iNO for > 4 hrs**, and/or treatment during hospitalization with **sildenafil** or other pulmonary vasodilatory medications for pulmonary hypertension.
- Congenital heart disease requiring surgery or minimally invasive intervention.
- History of observed clinical or electroencephalographic (EEF) seizure activity or receiving antiepileptic medication(s) at time of discharge.
- Evidence of intracranial pathology, including but not limited to, intracranial hemorrhage (grade II or worse), white matter injury including periventricular leukomalacia (PVL), cerebral thrombosis, cerebral infarction or stroke, congenital structural central nervous system (CNS) abnormality or other CNS problems associated with adverse neurologic outcome.
- Clinical history and/or physical exam findings consistent with neonatal encephalopathy.
- Other documented problems that could result in neurologic abnormality, such as: history of CNS infection, documented sepsis, bilirubin at excessive levels concerning for brain injury as determined by NICU medical staff (total pre-phototherapy bilirubin level >25mg/dL), history of cardiovascular instability as determined by NICU medical staff due to: sepsis, congenital heart disease, PDA, NEC, other documented conditions.

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.10 High-Risk Infant Follow-Up › Short list of criteria
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#short-list-of-criteria-1

Any of the following:

- Bilirubin > 25 mg/dL
- pH < 7.0
- Congenital heart defect requiring surgical correction
- Intubated for > 48 hrs

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.13 Discharge with Home Oxygen Use
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#sec-home-o2

The following steps may be followed to complete the process of discharge with home oxygen use:

1.  Contact Dr. Kyle McCallin (peds pulm) regarding discharge with home oxygen

2.  Dr. McCallin does not like to discharge on lower than 1/8 LPM (120 mL), 100% FiO2

3.  Per Dr. McCallin: obtain baseline gas, +/- repeat CXR if most recent CXR prior was abnormal (air trapping, diphragmatic flattening, atelectasis, etc.). Inquire if additional meds needed/ recommended (ie., pulmicort, albuterol, etc.)

4.  Contact NICU case manager (CM) to arrange DME order and delivery to patient’s home. CM at Moreno Valley can call the CM at Fontana for the details if s/he needs assistance.

5.  Once supplies have been delivered to the mother’s home, the baby may be discharged with a portable oxygen tank. (In this case, the supplier left mother with a large tank, a medium tank, and multiple small tanks).

6.  Ensure RT teaches mother proper usage of the tank or other supplies (ie. nebulizer)

7.  Peds pulm generally likes follow up 2-4 weeks post discharge, Dr. McCallin always wants the babies to see their actual PCP first.

**Keep in mind that this can be a rather quick process (<24 hours), so keep abreast of CM’s progress so as to be able to plan for the discharge accordingly**

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.14 Discharge Workflow on Health Connect › Step 1: Reconcile Medications
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#step-1-reconcile-medications

Go into the discharge navigator → Click on **MD med/order reconciliation** in left menu bar → Complete the medical reconciliation:

1.  Discontinue what is not needed, add whatever else infant needs as outpatient
2.  Order Poly-Vi-Sol for birth GA ≤ 37 weeks, or Poly-Vi-Sol with Iron for birh GA < 37 weeks
3.  Order **discharge diet** and enter [appropriate diet](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#sec-discharge-diet)
    - Make sure to copy/paste recipes for making 22kcal/oz or 24kcal/oz with Neosure or Enfacare 22kcal/oz to the Discharge Diet order
4.  Order **discharge activity** and enter the back to sleep information by using the dot-phrase [.back1](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/dotPhrases.html#sec-dotphrase-back1)
5.  Any other outpatient referrals

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.14 Discharge Workflow on Health Connect › Step 2: Reconcile Problem list
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#step-2-reconcile-problem-list

Resolve/update problems no longer relevant (ie. Receiving tube feedings)

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.14 Discharge Workflow on Health Connect › Step 3: Discharge summary
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#step-3-discharge-summary

Template:

- Use **DISCHARGE (CCS) SUMMARY NOTE NICU IP SCAL** in the smart text box for infants discharged home or transferred out of Kaiser foundation hospitals
- Use **TRANSFER OUTGOING TRANSFER NICU IP SCAL** for infants transferred to another Kaiser foundation hospital

Content:

- **Reason for admission**: copy H&P note from the beginning of note up to and including admission physical exam and paste here
- **Pertinent past hx**: usually just “newborn” is sufficient
- **Pertinent points in ROS**: from most recent progress note, copy and paste the assessment/plan section of all organ systems up to “social” (exclude the social history)
- **Update new and thorough physical exam**. Be sure to do eye exam and hip exam
- **Treatment/ care given**: CPAP, NIMV, antibiotics, etc (may use the dot-phrase [.treatment](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/@sect-dotPhrase-synopsis))
- **Brief Synopsis**: free text brief hospital course (may use the dot-phrase [.synopsis](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/@sec-dotPhrase-synopsis))
- **Discharge activity, diet and medications**: should all auto-populated if you completed these in Step 1 above
- Sign D/C summary

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.14 Discharge Workflow on Health Connect › Step 4: Route D/C Summary
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#step-4-route-dc-summary

Find your signed D/C summary within the Notes tab and click once:

1.  Find the **Route** icon to the right, on top of your D/C summary (in the same menu where **Addendum**, **Copy**, are found
2.  Route to PCP: Select **+ PCP** and the patient’s PCP will populate in the **send-to** section
3.  Route to the pediatric provider seeing baby after NICU discharge if not the PCP
4.  Cut and paste brief synopsis in comment section
5.  If infant’s birth GA <32 weeks or birth BW < 1,500g, D/C summary also needs to be routed to HRIF
    - Route D/C summary to **Nicole Ann Hutson** and **Pheba Joseph**
6.  If infant is a candidate for Synagis^®^, also route D/C summary to **Robyn Gerome**

If a patient is a candidate for IRC (see [Section 7.9](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#sec-irc)), place a **social work referral/consult** order on the day of discharge (this is an INPATIENT order)

## 7 Discharging an Infant › 7.14 Discharge Workflow on Health Connect › Step 9: HRIF follow up
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#step-9-hrif-follow-up

There is no need to order this and will be done automatically. Refer to the eligibility criteria in [Section 7.10](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#sec-hrif).

# 8 Documentation
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/documentation.html

## 8 Documentation › 8.1 Consent › Blood transfusion
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/documentation.html#sec-blood-transfusion

Prior to blood transfusion, a discussion between the neonatalogist and the parents must occur, followed by parental consenting. the discussion should be documented in the neonatologist’s admission H&P or daily progress note.

[dotPhrase](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/dotPhrases.html#sec-dotPhrase-blood-transfusion)

## 8 Documentation › 8.1 Consent › Donor breast milk
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/documentation.html#donor-breast-milk

Refer to [Section 12.10](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/nutrition-fluid.html#sec-donor-breast-milk) for details.

[dotPhrase](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/dotPhrases.html#sec-dotPhrase-donor-breast-milk)

## 8 Documentation › 8.1 Consent › Circumcision
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/documentation.html#circumcision

Refer to [Section 7.5](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/discharge.html#sec-circumcision) for details.

[dotPhrase](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/dotPhrases.html#sec-dotphrase-circconsent)

## 8 Documentation › 8.2 Daily Progress Note Component Reminder › PICC
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/documentation.html#sec-picc

When a PICC is in place and is working properly, document the following in the daily progress note:

> Central line/dates: peripherally inserted central catheter: date placed: MM/DD/YYYY. **PICC central line assessed today, is functioning well with no signs of inflammation. Plan: Will continue the use of the catheter fo rlong-term paretneral nutrition and/or blood draw.**

[dotPhrase](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/dotPhrases.html#sec-dotPhrase-picc)

## 8 Documentation › 8.2 Daily Progress Note Component Reminder › Skin-to-skin
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/documentation.html#sec-skin-to-skin

Parents are expected to do at least 1 hr of kangaroo care (skin-to-skin, STS) with the infant each day of their visit.

A [nursing communication order](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/dotPhrases.html#sec-dotphrase-skin-to-skin) should be placed on admission to encourage skin-to-skin, One an order is placed, it should be documented in the progress note as follows:

> **Skin-to-skin order placed**.

[dotPhrase](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/dotPhrases.html#sec-dotphrase-skin-to-skin)

## 8 Documentation › 8.3 Discharge Summary Component Reminder › TREATMENT/CARE GIVEN/COURSE OF HOSPITAL STAY
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/documentation.html#treatmentcare-givencourse-of-hospital-stay

Examples:

- Status post enteral and parenteral nutrition\
- Status post respiratory support for hyperMg and TTN
- CARDIORESPIRATORY EVENTS requiring Caffeine
- Status post antimicrobial therapy for rule out sepsis (blood c/s negative to date )
- CRANIAL ULTRASOUND negative\
- Status post phototherapy for non-hemolytic jaundice

## 8 Documentation › 8.3 Discharge Summary Component Reminder › Brief Synopsis
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/documentation.html#brief-synopsis

Example:

> 30.3 wks AGA male infant admitted to NICU on 9/18 via transfer from FKP for continuation of care for prematurity (high census) and received the following care:
>
> - NG feeding, Status post TPN/IL\
> - Anemia treated with Epogen and Iron\
> - Monitoring for cardiorespiratory events\
> - Status post circumcision\
> - History of antimicrobial therapy with negative Blood c/s at FKP\
> - History of respiratory support for TTN at FKP
>
> Post discharge follow up needs: nutrition using discharge diet of SSC 30 KCal/oz x 2 feedings/day, remainder mother’s bmilk 20 KCal/oz or Neosure 22KCal/oz, follow up with HIGH RISK INFANT F/U CLINIC/ INLAND REGIONAL, Dynamic hip US 6 wk - 4 months old, follow up Anemia.

## 8 Documentation › 8.4 Deferred Cord Clamping
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/documentation.html#deferred-cord-clamping

Deferred cord clamping (DCC) can be documented in the mother’s chart by going to the “**Delivery Summary**” tab before it is signed by an OB physician.

Below are screenshots from Health Connect to locate the DCC section:

Important

For multiple gestation, each infant has his/her own section to fill out.

## 8 Documentation › 8.5 Checking Oral Care Documentation
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/documentation.html#sec-oral-care

- Step 1: Click “Flowsheets” tab

- Step 2: Click “Shift NICU” tab

- Step 3: Open drop-down list “Safety/Identification/Care”

- Step 4: Click on “Care”

- Step 5: In “Hygiene” row, hover your mouse over boxes to see if “Oral Care w/ colostrum” was documented.

  **IMPORTANT**: It must include the “Oral Care w/ colostrum” for it to count for our metric. If it only says “Oral Care” but does not include “w/ colostrum,” it will not count.

- Step 6: document in progress note

## 8 Documentation › 8.6 Family and Medical Leave Act (FMLA) Document Preparation
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/documentation.html#sec-fmla

- Step 1: Click on arrow near the wrench for the drop down menu and locate “Activity Rx4”

- Step 2: Once on this page, select the “caregiver” button on the left

- Step 3: Fill out the appropriate dates you would like for FMLA, the requirements of the caregiver, and the diagnosis

- Step 4: Select “Go to Preview, Print, & Sign.” Print if the parent is requesting a copy. Otherwise, ok to just e-sign.

- Step 5: This process is then documented in the patient’s chart, where social work, case managent, etc. can locate the document ad lib:

# 9 Fetal and Neonatal Death Workflows and Documentation
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/death-workflows.html

## 9 Fetal and Neonatal Death Workflows and Documentation › 9.1 Executive summary › The issues we are trying to solve:
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/death-workflows.html#the-issues-we-are-trying-to-solve

1.  Each year there are a few dozen babies delivered at < 23 wk GA where the baby has died before delivery or dies shortly after birth.
2.  These are emotionally charged moments for families and we may upset them unintentionally through some of our practices. There are some “best practices” to share that help avoid those upsets.
3.  There is confusion around the many regulatory specifics for these babies. We wanted to provide some decision support for finding one’s way through those specifics.
4.  There are differences in expectation around the division of work and workflow between pediatricians and obstetricians and between nurses and physicians. We can clarify some of the general guidelines while recognizing the need to individualize roles in specific situations in the spirit collegiality and empathy to the family and team members.

## 9 Fetal and Neonatal Death Workflows and Documentation › 9.1 Executive summary › Key things we hope to change / improve in our practices:
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/death-workflows.html#key-things-we-hope-to-change-improve-in-our-practices

1.  Babies with signs of life are live born, regardless of gestational age
2.  “Uterogestational” age = gestational age in the usual sense (based on best obstetrical estimate EDC)
3.  Live born babies of any GA are pediatric patients though the expected bedside involvement of the pediatrician is very limited through well- established local OB/Neonatal workflows
    a.  Paperwork responsibilities: H&P, orders, discharge note, declaring death, death certificate
    b.  The NICU team usually will attend the delivery only if GA ≥ 22 wk
    c.  The obstetrician will manage the parents’ questions and concerns around the baby but the pediatrician will be available for specific concerns if requested by the obstetrician or parent
4.  The remains of pre-viable babies need to be treated with respect and sensitivity: including bundling, sending remains to morgue, avoiding handling as surgical path specimens except when specific studies are ordered
5.  Workflows: Local med center should develop and maintain OB/Neonatal workflows to maximize the quality and efficiency of care for all concerned (babies, mothers, pediatrician/neonatologists, OB/GYN, nursing, pathology, medical records, decedent affairs)

## 9 Fetal and Neonatal Death Workflows and Documentation › 9.1 Executive summary › Navigation of this document:
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/death-workflows.html#navigation-of-this-document

The document is divided into 4 sections:

2.  [“The fine print” on classifying deliveries in California (Live birth & fetal death definitions – State code)](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/death-workflows.html#sec-death-workflows-sec2)
3.  [Workflow and documentation FAQs](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/death-workflows.html#sec-death-workflows-sec3)
    a.  Workflow: Who does what at the bedside at delivery
    b.  Documentation: MD KPHC charting points on baby
    c.  Workflow: Disposition of remains issues after death
    d.  Documentation: Disposition of remains issues after death
4.  [Illustrative scenarios](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/death-workflows.html#sec-death-workflows-sec4)
5.  [Approval groups & committees](https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/death-workflows.html#sec-death-workflows-sec5)

## 9 Fetal and Neonatal Death Workflows and Documentation › 9.1 Executive summary › Section 1: Algorithm for Classifying & Documenting Deliveries in California
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/death-workflows.html#sec-death-workflows-sec1

``` {.mermaid .mermaid-js}
flowchart TD
  A["Signs of life at or after expulsion?<br><em>see live birth defined on next page<br>(HR OR limb movement or trunk movement or<br>respiratory muscle movement or pulsating cord,<br>regardless of gestational age)</em>"] --> B["Yes"]
  A --> C["No"]
  B --> D["Live birth"]
  D --> E["Died in first 30 days?"]
  E --> F["Yes"]
  F --> G["<span style='color:red;'>Neonatal Death</span>"]
  E --> H["No"]
  H --> I["Still Alive"]
  C --> J["Did the mother intend this<br>delivery to be classified as a<br>TAB/EAB?"]
  J --> K["No"]
  K --> L["GA at delivery? <em>(GA is the 'usual'<br>obstetrical estimate)</em>"]
  L --> M["<span style='color:red;'>Fetal demise GA < 20weeks</span>"]
  L --> N["<span style='color:red;'>Fetal demise GA ≥ 20weeks</span>"]
  J --> O["Yes"]
  O --> P["<span style='color:red;'>TAB</span>"]
```

## 9 Fetal and Neonatal Death Workflows and Documentation › 9.1 Executive summary › Section 2: “The Fine Print” on Classifying Deliveries in California (Live Birth & Fetal Death Definitions – State Code)
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/death-workflows.html#sec-death-workflows-sec2

- **Live birth defined**: “Live Birth” means the complete expulsion or extraction from its mother of a product of conception (irrespective of the duration of pregnancy) which, after such separation, breathes or shows any other evidence of life such as beating of the heart, pulsation of the umbilical cord, or definite movement of voluntary muscles, whether or not the umbilical cord has been cut or the placenta is attached. (California Code of Regulations, Title 17, Section 915).
- **Fetal death defined**: “Fetal death” means a death prior to complete expulsion or extraction from its mother of a product of conception (irrespective of the duration of pregnancy); the death is indicated by the fact that after such separation, the fetus does not breathe or show any other evidence of life such as beating of the heart, pulsation of the umbilical cord, or definite movement of voluntary muscles (referenced on 9/21/2018 from CDC summary of state regulations at <https://www.cdc.gov/nchs/data/misc/itop97.pdf>)

## 9 Fetal and Neonatal Death Workflows and Documentation › 9.1 Executive summary › Section 3: Workflow & Documentation FAQs › Workflow: Who Does What at the Bedside at Delivery?
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/death-workflows.html#workflow-who-does-what-at-the-bedside-at-delivery

| Activity or Documentation Required | TAB | Fetal Demise GA < 20 wk | Fetal Demise GA ≥ 20 wk | Live birth who dies (i.e. Neonatal Death) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Delivery summary (Stork) | Only if in L&D | Only if in L&D | Yes | Yes |
| Is Baby assigned MRN? | Only transiently: MRN assignment is required to make the Delivery Summary available for documentation. Selecting Fetal Demise in Delivery Summary will inactivate the MRN | Only transiently: MRN assignment is required to make the Delivery Summary available for documentation. Selecting Fetal Demise in Delivery Summary will inactivate the MRN | Only transiently: MRN assignment is required to make the Delivery Summary available for documentation. Selecting Fetal Demise in Delivery Summary will inactivate the MRN | Yes |
| Is KPHC chart created for baby? | No | No | No | Yes |
| Which physician is the attending physician in the baby’s care? | OB/GYN | OB/GYN | OB/GYN | Pediatrician/neonatologist |
| Who is attendance at delivery to care for baby? | OB team | OB team | OB team | NICU team generally only attends if GA > 22 wk but will attend on a case by cases basis at request of parent or a member of OB team |
| Where should baby be cared for till death? | Mother’s room | Mother’s room | Mother’s room | Per mother’s preference (Mother’s room or NICU) |
| Who declares death? | n/a | n/a | n/a | Pediatrician/neonatologist. Typically the RN will call the MD when baby’s heartbeat has ceased to beat. |

## 9 Fetal and Neonatal Death Workflows and Documentation › 9.1 Executive summary › Section 3: Workflow & Documentation FAQs › Documentation: MD KPHC Charting Points on Baby
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/death-workflows.html#documentation-md-kphc-charting-points-on-baby

| Activity or Documentation Required | TAB | Fetal Demise GA < 20 wk | Fetal Demise GA ≥ 20 wk | Live birth who dies (i.e. Neonatal Death) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Is baby admission and discharge note needed for baby? | No | No | No | Yes (admit and discharge note may be combined) |
| Are admission/discharge orders required for baby | No | No | No | Yes |
| If pathological exam desired, what type of exam for baby’s remains? | Surgical path order | Surgical path order | Autopsy | Autopsy |
| If pathological exam desired, is autopsy form needed? | No | No | Yes | Yes |

## 9 Fetal and Neonatal Death Workflows and Documentation › 9.1 Executive summary › Section 3: Workflow & Documentation FAQs › Workflow: Disposition of Remains Issues after Death
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/death-workflows.html#workflow-disposition-of-remains-issues-after-death

| Activity or Documentation Required | TAB | Fetal Demise GA < 20 wk | Fetal Demise GA ≥ 20 wk | Live birth who dies (i.e. Neonatal Death) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| How should the remains of a fetal demise or neonatal death be attired on labor and delivery floor? | Bundled (not in a surgical basin) | Bundled (not in a surgical basin) | Bundled (not in a surgical basin) | Bundled (not in a surgical basin) |
| Where should baby’s remains be stored in the pathology dept? | Usually to surgical specimen receiving Morgue if parents considering mortuary | Usually to surgical specimen receiving Morgue if parents considering mortuary | Morgue only | Morgue only |
| May family make private arrangements for remains? | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| May family leave remains to hospital for disposition? | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |

## 9 Fetal and Neonatal Death Workflows and Documentation › 9.1 Executive summary › Section 3: Workflow & Documentation FAQs › Documentation: Disposition of Remains Issues after Death
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/death-workflows.html#documentation-disposition-of-remains-issues-after-death

| Activity or Documentation Required | TAB | Fetal Demise GA < 20 wk | Fetal Demise GA ≥ 20 wk | Live birth who dies (i.e. Neonatal Death) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Is birth certificate required? | No | No | No | Yes |
| Is “Regular” “Certificate of Death” required? | No | No | No | Yes |
| Is “Fetal certificate of death” required? | No | No | Yes | No |
| If parents want to send remains to a mortuary, what paperwork is needed? | “Release to mortuary- coroner” form | “Release to mortuary- coroner” form | Release to mortuary- coroner” form | Release to mortuary-coroner” form |
| If parents want to have remains handled by hospital, what paperwork is needed? | “retain and dispose” form | “retain and dispose” form | Special county release form | Special county release form |

## 9 Fetal and Neonatal Death Workflows and Documentation › 9.1 Executive summary › Section 4: Illustrative Scenarios
URL: https://fschou-pxt.github.io/RMC-NICU-Manual/death-workflows.html#sec-death-workflows-sec4

| Scenario / Question | Answer |
| --- | --- |
| There was a crash C-section for bradycardia. The baby did not have a HR or movement at birth and did not respond to resuscitation. Is the baby a fetal death or live birth who failed resuscitation? | Fetal death |
| The pediatricians at our center haven’t been involved in periviable live births below a certain gestational age. How many babies might we be called to see if we now became involved? | The burden for neonatal physicians is very small and there will be little change in practice. Between 2010 and 2018 only about 35 babies were born annually in the whole Region. In 2018 all of the babies born alive at 22 wk GA were seen by pediatricians. The large majority of the 15 babies born alive at 20≤GA<22 wk were seen by pediatricians. Of the 11 babies born alive at GA < 20 wk no pediatricians were involved. |
| We haven’t consistently called the pediatrician low GA live births. If pediatricians are now called, will that inadvertently encourage resuscitations for babies that would otherwise be allowed to pass quietly? | The issue of whether or not to resuscitate a baby should be driven by careful discussions between the physicians and parents and not by the presence or absence of a neonatal team at delivery The choice of whether a neonatal team should attend a delivery should be part of those careful discussions A neonatal team’s presence at a delivery does not mandate resuscitation. The presence of the team, if called, is meant to ensure the most appropriate care is provided to the baby and assure the parents and non-neonatal staff that the appropriate level of support is being provided As with any delivery the pediatrician should respond to a request to come to the delivery room if requested by a member of the OB team as a matter of professional duty, whether or not a resuscitation will be performed |
| The baby made some arching truncal movements but there was no heart rate or signs of responsiveness. Is the baby a live born? | Yes. It is problematic in a number of ways to treat a baby some might consider alive as already dead. Any sign of life is treated by California state as evidence the baby is liveborn. It’s better to error on the side of calling a baby a live born when there is difference of opinion. |
| Does GA or BW change management of a baby with a heartbeat at birth? | No. There is no minimum GA or BW below which a baby with signs of life at birth is not treated as a live birth |
| Does GA or BW change management of TAB? | No. There is no maximum GA or BW for TAB |
| Does presence of FHR before delivery change above management? | No. It is the baby’s status at delivery that determines management |
| Is an induction at GA 24 wk for “inevitable abortion” that results in a fetal demise classified as a TAB? | No (unless that’s what the mother wants it to be categorized) |
| Is an 18 wk delivery with a HR for a few seconds classified as a miscarriage or a live birth? | Live birth. There is no minimum GA or BW below which a baby with signs of life at birth is not treated as a live birth. |
| What about a 10 wk delivery that barely looks like a baby but has a visible heartbeat? | Live birth unless the remains are not “recognizable anatomical parts” 7054.4. |
| I don’t think it’s humane to ask a family to fill out a birth certificate for an 18 wk death just because there was a visible heartbeat. What can I do? | The problem is that it is not technically legal to categorize the death after birth as a fetal demise. Convincing the family or nurses that a tiny baby with signs of life is not really alive is fraught for many reasons. So you need to devote your efforts to make this moment humane within the context of the law under these sad circumstances. The parents are not required to give the baby a first name |
| Spontaneous abortion, inevitable abortion, intrauterine fetal demise, stillbirth, miscarriage, fetal death and fetal demise, induced abortion: how do I decide which terms should be used? | “Fetal demise” is the only term used in “Stork”. “TAB” and “abortion”are the only terms used in the OB history. The other terms do not have strong consensus on definitions and can cause confusion |
| A mother presents with a spontaneous abortion in the ED or office: do I need to fill out a KPHC “delivery summary”? | No. The delivery summary is intended to be an inpatient document |
